<sup>13</sup> Therefore the Lord saith: Because this people draw near to me with their mouth, And honor me with their lips, And have removed their heart far from me, And their fear toward me hath been taught by the commandment of men;

<sup>14</sup> Therefore, behold! I add to do, A wonderful work among this people, A miracle And a prodigy; For the wisdom of their wise men shall perish, And the prudence of their prudent men shall vanish away.

<sup>15</sup> Wo to them that conceal themselves from Jehovah, That they may hide counsel; For their works are in the dark, And they say, Who seeth us? And, Who knoweth us?

<sup>16</sup> Is your turning reckoned like potters clay? Doth a work say of its author, He did not make me? And doth a thing framed say of its framer, He did not understand?

<sup>17</sup> Is it not yet a little, a little, And Lebanon shall be changed into Carmel, And Carmel shall be reckoned a forest?

<sup>18</sup> And in that day shall the deaf hear, The words of the book, And the eyes of the blind shall see, Out of obscurity And out of darkness.

<sup>19</sup> Then shall the humble again take joy in Jehovah, And the poor of men shall rejoice in the Holy One of Israel.

<sup>20</sup> For the violent man is brought to naught, The mocker is despised; And they who hastened early to iniquity are ruined.

<sup>21</sup> Who make a man to offend in word, Who have laid snares for him that reproveth in the gate, And have turned aside the righteous man for nothing.

<sup>22</sup> Therefore Jehovah, who redeemed Abraham, Speaketh thus to the house of Jacob: Jacob shall not now be confounded. Nor shall his face now become pale.

<sup>23</sup> For when he shall see in the midst of him His children, the work of my hands, They shall sanctify my name, They shall sanctify the Holy One of Jacob, They shall fear the God of Israel.

<!-- PAGE 711 -->

<sup>24</sup> Then they that erred in spirit shall learn understanding, And the murmurers shall learn doctrine.

<sup>1</sup> Wo to the rebellious <span style="color:#800000">fa47</span> children, saith Jehovah, That they may take counsel. And not from me; That they may cover a secret, <span style="color:#800000">fa48</span> And not from my Spirit; That they may add sin to sin.

<sup>2</sup> Who set out that they may go down into Egypt, And have not inquired at my mouth; Strengthening themselves with the strength of Pharaoh, And trusting in the shadow of Egypt.

<sup>3</sup> But the strength of Pharaoh, Shall become to you shame; And reliance on the shadow of Egypt, Shall become disgrace.

<sup>4</sup> For his princes were in Zoan, And his ambassadors came to Hanes.

<sup>5</sup> All shall be ashamed, Of a people that will not profit them, And will not become a help, And will not yield advantage, But will become shame, And even disgrace.

<sup>6</sup> The burden of the beasts of the south. In the land of trouble And anguish, The lion And the stronger lion, The viper And the flying serpent; While they shall carry their riches On the shoulders of young asses, And on the bunches of camels, To a people that will not profit them.

<sup>7</sup> Surely the Egyptians are vanity, And shall help in vain. Therefore have I cried to her, Their strength is to sit still.

<sup>8</sup> Now go, And write this vision on a tablet before them, And engrave it in a book, That it may be till the last day, For ever And ever.

<sup>9</sup> For this is a rebellious people, Lying children, Children who refuse to hear the law of Jehovah.

<!-- PAGE 712 -->

<sup>10</sup> Who say to the seers, Do not see, And to the foreseers, Do not foresee to us right things; Speak ye to us flatteries, See ye errors.

<sup>11</sup> Go out of the way; Turn aside from the path; Cause the Holy One of Israel To depart from our presence.

<sup>12</sup> Therefore thus saith the Holy One of Israel: Because ye have disdained this word, And have trusted in violence And wickedness, And have rested on it;

<sup>13</sup> Therefore shall your iniquity be to you, Like a breach falling, Like the bulging out in a lofty wall, The fall of which comes suddenly And unexpectedly.

<sup>14</sup> And the breaking of it shall be, As the breaking of a potters vessel, Which is broken without mercy; And in its breaking there is not found a sherd To carry fire from the hearth, Or to draw water from a well.

<sup>15</sup> For thus saith the Lord Jehovah, The Holy One of Israel: “In rest And quietness shall you be safe; In peace And confidence shall be your strength; But ye would not.

<sup>16</sup> And ye said, ]No, but we will flee on horses; Therefore shall ye flee; We will ride on the swift; Therefore they that shall pursue you shall be more swift.

<sup>17</sup> A thousand as one from the face of the rebuke of one, From the face of the rebuke of five shall ye flee, Until ye shall be left, As the mast of a ship on the top of a mountain. And as a banner on a hill.

<sup>18</sup> Therefore will Jehovah wait for you, That he may have compassion on you; And therefore will he be exalted, That he may have compassion on you; For Jehovah is a God of judgment: Blessed are all they that wait for him.

<!-- PAGE 713 -->

<sup>19</sup> Surely the people in Zion shall dwell in Jerusalem: Weeping thou shalt not weep; With compassion will he have compassion on thee; At the voice of thy cry, as soon as he shall hear it, He will answer thee.

<sup>20</sup> When the Lord shall have given to you, Bread of anguish, And water of affliction, Thy rain shall not be withheld, And thine eyes shall see thy rain.

<sup>21</sup> Then shall thine ears hear a word behind thee, Saying, This is the way; Walk ye in it; another ye go to the right, Or go to the left.

<sup>22</sup> Then shall ye profane, The covering of the graven images of thy silver, And the covering of thy molten gold; And thou shalt put it away from thee as a menstruous cloths. And shalt say to it, Depart. <span style="color:#800000">fa49</span> <sup>23</sup> Then will he give rain to thy seed, When thou shalt have sowed the ground, And bread of the produce of the earth; And it shall be plentiful And fat; And in that day thy cattle Shall feed in large pastures.

<sup>24</sup> Thine oxen also, And the young asses that labor the ground, Shall eat clean provender, Which shall be winnowed with the shovel, And with the sieve.

<sup>25</sup> And it shall come to pass, That on every high mountain, And on every lofty hill, Shall be streams, streams of waters, In the day of the great slaughter When the towers shall have fallen.

<sup>26</sup> And the light of the moon shall be as the light of the sun, And the light of the sun shall be sevenfold, As the light of seven days, In the day when Jehovah, Shall have bound up the breach of his people, And shall have healed the stroke of their wound.

<sup>27</sup> Behold! the name of Jehovah cometh From a distant place: His face burneth, And the burden is heavy; His lips are full of indignation, And his tongue as a devouring fire.

<!-- PAGE 714 -->

<sup>28</sup> And his breath, as an overflowing torrent, Shall divide even to the neck; To sift the nations with a useless sieve; And on the cheeks of the peoples There shall be a bridle causing to err.

<sup>29</sup> You shall have a song, as in the night When a festival is kept. And gladness of heart, As of him. that moveth to a pipe, That he may come to the mountain of Jehovah, To the Mighty One of Israel.

<sup>30</sup> And Jehovah shall cause to be heard the power of his voice, And shall cause to be seen the descent of his arm, With rage of countenance, And with the flame of devouring fire, With scattering, with flood, And with hailstones.

<sup>31</sup> Verily by the voice of Jehovah shall the Assyrian be crushed, Who smote with a rod.

<sup>32</sup> And in every passage shall be the fastened staff, Which Jehovah shall lay upon him With tabrets And harps, And with battles of lifting up Shall he fight against her.

<sup>33</sup> For Prophet is ordained since yesterday; Yea, for the king it is prepared: He hath made it deep And large; The pile of it is fire, And much wood; The breath of Jehovah, like a torrent of brimstone, Doth kindle it.

<sup>1</sup> Wo to them that go clown into Egypt for help, And who rely on horses, And who trust to chariots, because they are numerous, And to horses, because they are very strong, And have not looked to the Holy One of Israel, Nor have sought Jehovah.

<sup>2</sup> Yet he also is wise: Therefore he will bring evil, And will not make void his words; he will rise up against the house of the evil-doers, And against the aid of the workers of vanity.

<!-- PAGE 715 -->

<sup>3</sup> And verily the Egyptian is a man, And not God; And his horses are flesh, And not spirit. Therefore, as soon as Jehovah shall stretch out his arm, The helper shall fall, And he who is helped shall fall down, And all shall fail together.

<sup>4</sup> For thus hath Jehovah said to me: As the lion roareth, And the young lion for his prey, Against whom, if a multitude of shepherds be gathered together, He shall not be alarmed by their cry, And shall not humble himself on account of their noise; Thus will Jehovah of hosts come down To fight for Mount Zion, And for its hill.

<sup>5</sup> As birds which fly, So will Jehovah of hosts defend Jerusalem; Defending, he will deliver it, And passing over, he will preserve it.

<sup>6</sup> Return ye, As ye have made a deep revolt, O children of Israel <sup>7</sup> For in that day shall a man cast away The idols of his silver, And the idols of his gold, Which your hands have made for you, a sin.

<sup>8</sup> Then shall the Assyrian fall by the sword, not of a mighty man; And the sword, not of a man, shall devour him; And by flight shall he seek safety from the face of the sword, And his young men shall melt away.

<sup>9</sup> He shall pass to his stronghold through fear, And his princes shall tremble at the banner, saith Jehovah, Who hath a fire <span style="color:#800000">fa50</span> in Zion, And who hath a furnace in Jerusalem.

<sup>1</sup> Behold! a king shall reign in righteousness, And princes shall rule in judgment.

<sup>2</sup> And that man shall be, As a hiding-place from the wind, As a covert from the rainy, As streams of waters on dry ground, As the shadow of a great rock in a weary land.

<!-- PAGE 716 -->

<sup>3</sup> Then the eyes of them that see shall not be closed up, And the ears of them that hear shall hearken.

<sup>4</sup> And the heart of fools shall be eagerly directed to knowledge, And the tongue of stammerers shall be ready to speak plainly.

<sup>5</sup> No longer shall the base man be called liberal, Nor shall the parsimonious man be called bountiful.

<sup>6</sup> For the vile person will speak vileness, And his heart will contrive iniquity, To commit wickedness, That he may utter mockery against Jehovah, That he may make empty the hungry soul, And that he may withhold drink from him that is thirsty.

<sup>7</sup> The instruments of the covetous man are evil; <span style="color:#800000">fa51</span> He contriveth wickednesses, That he may deceive the simple by lying words, And that he may speak against the poor man in judgment.

<sup>8</sup> But the liberal man shall devise liberal things, And in acting liberally he shall make progress.

<sup>9</sup> Ye women at ease, arise; Hear my voice, ye careless daughters; Hearken to my speech.

<sup>10</sup> Days above a year shall ye tremble, ye careless women; For the vintage shall fail, And the gathering shall not come.

<sup>11</sup> Tremble, ye women that are at ease; Be troubled, ye careless women; Strip you, make you bare, gird your limbs.

<sup>12</sup> Mourning over the udders, Over the pleasant fields, Over the fruitful vine.

<sup>13</sup> There shall come up t. he thorn And brier, On the land of my people, Even on all the houses of gladness in the city of rejoicing.

<sup>14</sup> For the palace shall be forsaken; The noise of the city shall be left; The tower And the fortress, Shall be reduced to dens for ever, Where wild asses may delight themselves, And where flocks may feed.

<!-- PAGE 717 -->

<sup>15</sup> Till the Spirit be poured out upon you from on high, And the wilderness become a cultivated field, And the cultivated field be reckoned like a forest.

<sup>16</sup> And judgment shall dwell in the wilderness, And justice shall have its abode in the cultivated field.

<sup>17</sup> And the work of righteousness shall be peace; The effect of righteousness shall be Safety And quietness for ever.

<sup>18</sup> And my people shall dwell in a tabernacle of peace, And in safe dwellings, And in quiet resting-places.

<sup>19</sup> And in coming down the hail shall turn aside on the forest, And the city shall be situated in a low place.

<sup>20</sup> Blessed are ye who sow on all waters, Who send forth the feet of the ox And of the ass.

<sup>1</sup> Wo to thee that spoilest, And was not spoiled; Who dealest wickedly, And they dealt not wickedly with thee! When thou shalt have ceased to spoil, Thou shalt be spoiled; When thou shalt have ceased to deal wickedly, They shall deal wickedly with thee.

<sup>2</sup> O Jehovah, have pity upon us; We have hoped in thee; Be thou, who hast been their arm in the morning, Our salvation also in the time of trouble.

<sup>3</sup> At the voice of the tumult the people fled; At thy exaltation the nations were dispersed.

<sup>4</sup> And your prey shall be gathered by your gathering of caterpillars, Advancing thither, according to the running of locusts.

<sup>5</sup> Jehovah is exalted, who dwelleth on high; He hath filled Zion with judgment And righteousness.

<!-- PAGE 718 -->

<sup>6</sup> And the stability of thy times shall be Strength, salvation, wisdom, And knowledge; The fear of Jehovah is his treasure.

<sup>7</sup> Behold, their messengers shall cry without; The ambassadors of peace shall weep bitterly.

<sup>8</sup> The roads are deserted, The traveler hath ceased; He hath violated the treaty, He hath despised the cities, He hath paid no regard to any man.

<sup>9</sup> The earth hath mourned And languished; Lebanon is ashamed And hewn down; Sharon hath been made like a wilderness; Bashan And Carmel have been shaken.

<sup>10</sup> Now will I arise, saith Jehovah; Now will I be exalted; Now will I lift up myself.

<sup>11</sup> Ye shall conceive chaff; Ye shall bring forth stubble; The fire of your breath shall devour you.

<sup>12</sup> And the peoples shall be the burnings of lime; <span style="color:#800000">fa52</span> As thorns cut up shall they be burnt in the fire.

<sup>13</sup> Hear, ye that are far off, what I have done; Ye that are near, acknowledge my power.

<sup>14</sup> The sinners in Zion are afraid; Terror hath seized the hypocrites. <span style="color:#800000">fa53</span> Which of us <span style="color:#800000">fa54</span> shall dwell with devouring fire? Which of us shall dwell with everlasting burnings?

<sup>15</sup> He who walketh in righteousnesses And speaketh what is right; Who despiseth the gain from violence And calumny; Who shaketh his hands from accepting a bribe; Who stoppeth his ear, that he may not hear blood; Who shutteth his eyes, that he may not see evil.

<sup>16</sup> He shall dwell in high places; Fortifications of rocks shall be his refuge; Bread shall be given to him, And his waters (shall be) sure.

<sup>17</sup> Thine eyes shall see the king in his beauty; They shall behold the land (that is) very far off.

<!-- PAGE 719 -->

<sup>18</sup> Thine heart shall meditate terror. Where is the scribe? where is the weigher? Where is he who singleth out the principal houses?

<sup>19</sup> The fierce people thou shalt not see, The people of a confused lip, so that thou canst not understand; Of a stammering tongue, so that thou canst not comprehend.

<sup>20</sup> Look at Zion, the city of our solemnities; <span style="color:#800000">fa55</span> Thine eyes shall see Jerusalem a peaceful habitation, A tent which shall not be carried away, The stakes of which shall never be removed, And of which none of the ropes shall be broken:

<sup>21</sup> Thus, <span style="color:#800000">fa56</span> shall the mighty Jehovah there be to us, A place of streams, of broad rivers, Through which there shall not pass a ship with oars, And through which a splendid ship shall not pass.

<sup>22</sup> For Jehovah is our Judge; Jehovah is our Lawgiver; Jehovah is our King; He himself will save us.

<sup>23</sup> Thy cords were loosed, So that they did not fasten their mast, Nor spread the sail; Then was ; he prey of much spoil divided, The lame also seized the prey.

<sup>24</sup> And the inhabitant shall not say, I am sick; The people that dwell in it have been freed from iniquity.

<sup>1</sup> Draw near, ye nations, to hear; And ye peoples, hearken. Let the earth hear, And its fullness; The world, And all its productions.

<sup>2</sup> For the indignation of Jehovah is on all nations, And his fury on all their army; He hath destroyed them; He hath delivered them to slaughter.

<sup>3</sup> Their slain shall be east out; And from their carcasses shall come up their stench; And the mountains shall melt on account of their blood.

<!-- PAGE 720 -->

<sup>4</sup> And all the armies of heaven shall fade away, And shall be rolled up as a scroll. The heavens And all their armies shall fall down, As a leaf falleth down from a vine, And as it falleth from the figtree.

<sup>5</sup> For my sword is made drunken in the heavens. Behold, it shall come down on Edom, Even on the people of my curse to judgment.

<sup>6</sup> The sword of Jehovah hath been filled with blood; It hath been made fat with fatness; Even with the blood of lambs And of goats, With the fat of the kidneys of rams; For the sacrifice of Jehovah is on Bozrah, And a great slaughter in the land of Edom.

<sup>7</sup> And the unicorns shall come down with them, And the bullocks with the bulls; And their land shall be made drunken with blood, And their dust shall be made fat with fatness.

<sup>8</sup> For (it is) the day of vengeance of Jehovah, The year of recompenses for the cause of Zion.

<sup>9</sup> And her streams shall be turned into pitch, And her dust into brimstone; And her land shall become burning pitch.

<sup>10</sup> By night And by day it shall not be quenched; Its smoke shall continually go up; From generation to generation it shall lie waste; Tone shall pass through it for ever And ever.

<sup>11</sup> Therefore shall the pelican And the owl take possession of it; The great owl And the raven shall dwell in it; And he shall stretch over it the cord of emptiness, And the plummets of vanity.

<sup>12</sup> They shall call her nobles without a kingdom, And all her princes shall be nothing.

<sup>13</sup> In her palaces she shall bring forth thorns, And nettles And thistles in her fortresses; And she shall be a habitation for dragons, And an abode for the young ones of the ostrich.

<!-- PAGE 721 -->

<sup>14</sup> And the wild beasts shall meet with the satyrs; And the screech-owl shall cry to his companion; There also shall the fairy dwell, And shall find for herself a peaceful abode.

<sup>15</sup> There shall the owl make her nest, And lay, And hatch And gather her young under her shadow; Yea, there shall the vultures be gathered together, Every one with her mate.

<sup>16</sup> Inquire at the book of Jehovah, And read. Not one of those shall fail; Tot one shall want her mate; For his mouth hath commanded, And his Spirit hath gathered them.

<sup>17</sup> And he hath cast the lot for them; And his hand hath divided it to them for ever, as by a cord. Therefore they shall inhabit it; From generation to generation they shall dwell in it.

<sup>1</sup> The wilderness And the desert shall be glad; The loathsome place shall rejoice, And shall flourish like the lily.

<sup>2</sup> Flourishing it shall flourish, And shall even rejoice with rejoicing, And shall sing; The glory of Lebanon shall be given to it, The beauty of Carmel And Sharon; They shall see the glory of Jehovah, The majesty of our God.

<sup>3</sup> Strengthen ye the weak hands, Confirm the tottering knees.

<sup>4</sup> Say to them that are trembling in heart, Be strong, fear not. Behold, your God will come with vengeance; God himself will come with a recompense, <span style="color:#800000">fa57</span> And will save you.

<sup>5</sup> Then shall the eyes of the blind be opened, And the ears of the deaf shall be opened.

<sup>6</sup> Then shall the lame man leap like a stag, The tongue of the dumb mall shall sound loudly; For waters shall be digged in the desert, And rivers in the wilderness.

<!-- PAGE 722 -->

<sup>7</sup> The dry place shall be changed into a pool, And the parched country into fountains of waters; And in the habitation of dragons, And in its lair, Shall be a place for the reed And the rush.

<sup>8</sup> And a path shall be there, And a way, And it shall be called, The holy way. An unclean person shall not pass through it; And he shall be to them one that walketh in the way, That fools may not go astray.

<sup>9</sup> There shall not be there a lion; Nor shall a wild beast go up by it; Neither shall it be found there; That the redeemed may walk.

<sup>10</sup> Therefore shall the redeemed of Jehovah return, And shall come to Zion with rejoicing; And everlasting joy shall be on their head; And they shall obtain joy And gladness; And sorrow And sighing shall flee away.

<sup>1</sup> It came to pass in the fourteenth year of king Hezekiah, that Sennacherib, king of Assyria, came up against all the defended cities of Judah, And took them.

<sup>2</sup> Then the king of Assyria sent Rabshakch from Lachish, with a powerful army, to Jerusalem, to king Hezekiah, who stood at the conduit of the upper pool in the way of the fullers field.

<sup>3</sup> And there came out to him Eliakim, son of Hilkiah, who was over the house, And Shebna the chancellor, And Joab, the son of Asaph, the secretary.

<sup>4</sup> And Rabshakeh said to them: Say now to Hezekiah? Thus saith the great king, the king of Assyria, What confidence is this in which thou trustest? I have said, (only a word of the lips.)

<sup>5</sup> Counsel And strength for war. Now in whom hast thou trusted, that thou hast rebelled against me?

<!-- PAGE 723 -->

<sup>6</sup> Behold, thou hast rusted in that broken staff of reed, on Egypt, on which if one lean, it will go into his hand And pierce it. Such is Pharaoh, king of Egypt, to all who have confidence in him.

<sup>7</sup> And if thou shalt say to me, We trust in Jehovah our God; is it not, he whose high places And altars Hezekiah hath taken away, And hath said to Judah And Jerusalem, Ye shall worship before this altar?

<sup>8</sup> Now, come, give a hostage to my master, the king of Assyria. I will give thee two thousand horses; wilt thou have horsemen to set upon them?

<sup>9</sup> And how dost thou despise the face of one captain of the least of my masters servants, And place thy confidence in Egypt for chariots And horsemen?

<sup>10</sup> And have I now come up without Jehovah to this land, to destroy it? Jehovah said to me: Go up against this land, to destroy it.

<sup>11</sup> Then said Eliakim, And Shebna, And Joah, to Rabshakeh: Speak, I pray, to thy servants, in the Syrian language, (for we understand it,) And speak not to us in the Jewish language, in the hearing of the people who are on the wall.

<sup>12</sup> And Rabshakeh said: Hath my master sent me to thy master And to thee to speak those words? Is it not to the men who sit on the wall, that they may eat their own dung, And drink their own urine with you?

<sup>13</sup> Therefore Rabshakeh stood, And cried with a loud voice in the Jewish language, And said: Hear the words of the great king, the king of Assyria.

<sup>14</sup> Thus saith the king, Let not Hezekiah impose upon you, for he will not be able to deliver you.

<sup>15</sup> And let not Hezekiah make you trust in Jehovah, saying, By delivering will Jehovah deliver us; this city shall not be given up into the hand of the king of Assyria.

<!-- PAGE 724 -->

<sup>16</sup> Listen not to Hezekiah; for thus saith the king of Assyria, Make with me a blessing, And come out; And let every one eat of his own vine, And let every one eat of his own fig-tree, And let every one drink the waters of his own well.

<sup>17</sup> Till I come And take you into a land like your own land, a land of corn And wine, a land of bread And vineyards.

<sup>18</sup> Lest perhaps Hezekiah deceive you, saying, Jehovah will deliver us. Have any of the gods of the nations delivered their land out of the hand of the king of Assyria?

<sup>19</sup> Where is the god of Hamath And Arpad? Where is the god of Sepharvaim? Have they delivered Samaria out of my hand?

<sup>20</sup> Who is there among all the gods of those lands that hath delivered his land out of my hand; that Jehovah should rescue Jerusalem out of my hand?

<sup>21</sup> They were silent, And did not answer him a word; for this was the commandment of the king, Do not answer him.

<sup>22</sup> Then came Eliakim, who was over the house And Shebna the chancellor, And Joab, the son of Asaph, the secretary, to Hezekiah, with rent garments, And told him the words of Rabshakeh.

<sup>1</sup> Now, it came to pass, that Hezekiah, when he heard this rent his clothes, And covered himself with sackcloth, And went into the house of Jehovah.

<sup>2</sup> At the same time he sent Eliakim, who was over the palace, And Shebna the chancellor, and the eldest of the priests, covered with sackcloth, to Isaiah the son of Amoz, the prophet.

<sup>3</sup> Who said to him, Thus saith Hezekiah, This is a day of trouble, and of rebuke, And of blasphemy; for the children have come to the birth, and there is not strength to bring forth.

<!-- PAGE 725 -->

<sup>4</sup> If, perhaps, Jehovah thy God will hear the words of Rabshakeh, whom his master, the king of Assyria, hath sent to curse the living God, And to rebuke with words which Jehovah thy God hath heard. Thou shalt therefore lift up a prayer for the remnant that is still left.

<sup>5</sup> The servants of King Hezekiah came to Isaiah.

<sup>6</sup> And Isaiah said to them, Thus shall ye say to your master: Thus saith Jehovah, Fear not the words which thou hast heard, with which the servants of the king of Assyria have reproached me.

<sup>7</sup> Behold, I will bring a wind upon him; for he shall hear a report, And shall return to his own land; And I will cause him to fall by the sword in his own land.

<sup>8</sup> And Rabshakeh, having returned, found the king of Assyria besieging Libnah; for he had heard that he was departed from Lachish.

<sup>9</sup> And hearing concerning Tirhakah, king of Ethiopia, He hath gone out to fight against thee; after having heard it, he sent messengers to Hezekiah, saying:

<sup>10</sup> Thus shall ye say to Hezekiah king of Judah: Let not thy God, in whom thou trustest, deceive thee, saying, Jerusalem shall not be given up into the hand of the king of Assyria.

<sup>11</sup> Behold, thou hast heard what the kings of Assyria have done to at hands, how they have destroyed them; And shalt thou be delivered?

<sup>12</sup> Have the gods of the nations delivered those whom my fathers destroyed, Gozan, And Itaran, And Rezeph, And the children of Edom, who were in Telassar?

<sup>13</sup> Where is the king of Hamath, the king of Arphad, the king of the city of Sepharvaim, of Hena And Iva?

<!-- PAGE 726 -->

<sup>14</sup> Hezekiah received the letter from the hand of the messengers, And read it, And went up to the house of Jehovah, and spread it before Jehovah.

<sup>15</sup> Then Hezekiah prayed to God, saying:

<sup>16</sup> O Jehovah of hosts, God of Israel, who dwellest between the cherubim, thou alone art God over all the kingdoms of the earth; thou hast made heaven And earth.

<sup>17</sup> Incline thine ear, O Jehovah, And hear; open thine eyes, O Jehovah, And see; And hear all the words of Sennacherib, who hath sent to reproach the living God.

<sup>18</sup> Truly, O Jehovah, the kings of Assyria have destroyed all the nations, And their land, <sup>19</sup> And have cast their gods into the fire; for they were not gods, but the work of men’s hands, wood, And stone; therefore they have destroyed them.

<sup>20</sup> And now, O Jehovah our God, save us from his hand; that all the kingdoms of the earth may know that thou alone art Jehovah.

<sup>21</sup> Then Isaiah, the son of Amoz, sent to Hezekiah, saying: Thus saith Jehovah, the God of Israel, Since thou hast prayed to me concerning Sennacherib, king of Assyria;

<sup>22</sup> This is the word which Jehovah hath spoken concerning him: The virgin daughter of Zion hath despised thee, hath laughed at thee; The daughter of Jerusalem hath shaken her head at thee. Whom hast thou reproached?

<sup>23</sup> And whom hast thou blasphemed? Against whom hast thou exalted thy Voice, And lifted up thy hands on high? Even against the Holy One of Israel.

<!-- PAGE 727 -->

<sup>24</sup> By the hand of thy servants thou hast reproached the Lord, And hast said, By the multitude of my chariots I will go up, To the heights of the mountains, To the sides of Lebanon; I will cut down the tallest of her cedars, Her choice firs; Then will I come to the height of his border, And even to his level forest. <span style="color:#800000">fa58</span> <sup>25</sup> I will dig, And will drink waters; With the sole of my feet I will dry up all the lakes of the siege.

<sup>26</sup> Hast thou not heard that I made it long ago, That I formed it from ancient days? And should I now bring it to be a desolation, To be heaps of ruins, like fortified cities?

<sup>27</sup> For their inhabitants were maimed, Were terrified And confounded; They were made like the grass of the field And the green herb, Like the grass of the house-tops, which withereth before it is ripe.

<sup>28</sup> I know thy sitting down, And thy going out, And thy entrance, And thy indignation against me.

<sup>29</sup> Because thou wast angry against me, Thy tumult hath come up into my ears. Therefore will I put my hook <span style="color:#800000">fa59</span> in thy nostril, And my bridle ill thy lips, And will turn thee back by the way by which thou camest.

<sup>30</sup> And this shall be a sign to thee; Thou shalt eat this year that which groweth of itself, And in the second year that which springeth up of itself; And in the third year ye shall sow And reap, And shall plant vineyards, And eat the fruit of them.

<sup>31</sup> And that which shall be preserved of the house of Judah, And that which shall be left, Shall vet strike root downward, And shall bear fruit upward.

<sup>32</sup> For out of Jerusalem shall go forth a remnant, And that which shall be preserved of Mount Zion. The zeal of Jehovah of hosts will do this.

<!-- PAGE 728 -->

<sup>33</sup> Therefore thus saith Jehovah concerning the king of Assyria, He shall not enter into this city, Nor throw an arrow into it; And he who is defended by a shield shall not seize it, Nor cast a balister against it.

<sup>34</sup> By the way that he came shall he return, And shall not enter into this city, saith Jehovah.

<sup>35</sup> And I will be a protector to this city, To save it, for my own sake, And for the sake of my servant David.

<sup>36</sup> And the angel of Jehovah went forth, And smote in the camp of the Assyrians a hundred And eighty-five thousand; And when the people arose early in the morning, behold, they were all corpses of dead men.

<sup>37</sup> Then Sennacherib, king of Assyria, departed, And went, And returned, And dwelt in Nineveh.

<sup>38</sup> And it came to pass, while he was worshipping his god in the temple of Nisroch, <span style="color:#800000">fa60</span> that his sons, Adrammeleeh And Sharezar, smote him with the sword, And fled into the land of Armenia; And Esarhaddon his son reigned in his stead.

<sup>1</sup> In those days was Hezekiah sick unto death. And Isaiah the prophet, the son of Amoz, came to him, And said to him: Thus saith Jehovah, Give charge concerning thy house; <span style="color:#800000">fa61</span> for thou shalt die, And shalt not live.

<sup>2</sup> Then Hezekiah turned his face to the wall, And prayed to Jehovah.

<!-- PAGE 729 -->
<sup>3</sup> And he said, I beseech thee, O Jehovah, remember now that I have walked before thee in truth, And with a perfect heart, And have done what was right in thine eyes. And Hezekiah wept with sore weeping. <span style="color:#800000">fa62</span> <sup>4</sup> Then was communicated the word of Jehovah to Isaiah, saying, <sup>5</sup> Go, And say to Hezekiah: Thus saith Jehovah, the God of Jacob thy father: I have heard thy prayer, And have seen thy tears: Behold, I add to thy days fifteen years.

<sup>6</sup> And I will deliver thee And this city out of the hand of the king of Assyria; And I will be a protector to this city.

<sup>7</sup> And this shall be a sign to thee from Jehovah, that Jehovah will do this thing, of which he hath spoken:

<sup>8</sup> Behold, I bring back the shadow of degrees, by which it hath gone down on the sun-dial of Ahaz, ten degrees. And he sun went back on the sundial ten degrees, by which it had already gone down.

<sup>9</sup> The writing of Hezekiah, king of Judah, when he had been sick, And had recovered from his sickness.

<sup>10</sup> I said in the cutting off of my days, I shall go to the gates of the grave; I am deprived of the residue of my years.

<sup>11</sup> I said, I shall not see God, God in the land of the living; I shall no longer behold man with the inhabitants of the world.

<sup>12</sup> Sly habitation is departed, And is removed from me as a shepherds tent; I have cut off; as a weaver, my life: From lifting up <span style="color:#800000">fa63</span> he will cut me off; From day even to night wilt thou make an end of me.

<sup>13</sup> I reckoned <span style="color:#800000">fa64</span> till the dawn; As a lion, so he hath broken my bones; From the dawn till night thou wilt make an end of me.

<sup>14</sup> As a crane or a swallow, I chattered; I mourned as a dove. My eyes were lifted up on high, O Lord, it hath oppressed me; comfort me.

<sup>15</sup> What shall I say? He who hath spoken to me hath done it. I shall be moved <span style="color:#800000">fa65</span> all the days of my life In the bitterness of my soul.

<!-- PAGE 730 -->

<sup>16</sup> O Lord, by all who shall live beyond those (years) Shall the life of my spirit (be known;) And that thou didst cause me to sleep, And didst make me alive.

<sup>17</sup> Behold, in peace my bitterness was bitter; And thou hast been pleased (to rescue) my soul from the pit; <span style="color:#800000">fa66</span> For thou hast east behind thy back all my sins.

<sup>18</sup> For hell shall not confess thee; Death shall not praise thee; They that go down into the pit shall not wait for thy truth.

<sup>19</sup> The living, the living, he shall confess to thee, As I (do) this day; The father to the sons shall make known thy truth.

<sup>20</sup> Jehovah to save me; And we will sing our songs All the days of our life in the house of Jehovah.

<sup>21</sup> Now, Isaiah had said, They shall take a mass of figs, and apply it to the boil, And they shall live.

<sup>22</sup> For <span style="color:#800000">fa67</span> Hezekiah had said, What is the sign that I shall go up into the house of the Lord?

<sup>1</sup> At that time Merodach-Baladan, son of Baladan, king of Babylon, sent letters And a present to Hezekiah, after having heard that he had been sick, And was recovered.

<sup>2</sup> And Hezekiah was glad on account of them, And showed to them the house of his treasure, silver, And gold, And spices, precious ointment, And all his armory, And all that was contained in his treasures; there was not anything in his house, And in all his kingdom, which Hezekiah did not show.

<sup>3</sup> Then came Isaiah the prophet to king Pezekiah, And said to him, What did those men say. And whence came they to thee. Hezekiah answered, They came to me from a distant country, from Babylon.

<!-- PAGE 731 -->

<sup>4</sup> Then he said, What have they seen in thy house? And Hezekiah said, They have seen all that is in my house; And there is nothing in my treasures which I have not showed to them.

<sup>5</sup> Then Isaiah said to Hezekiah, Hear the word of Jehovah of hosts:

<sup>6</sup> Behold, the days come, that all that is in thy house, And all that thy fathers have treasured up till this day, shall be carried to Babylon; And nothing shall be left, saith Jehovah.

<sup>7</sup> Of thy sons which shall issue from thee, whom thou shalt beget, shall they take; And they shall be eunuchs in the palace of the king of Babylon.

<sup>8</sup> Hezekiah said to Isaiah, Good is the word of Jehovah which thou hast spoken. And he said, At least there shall be peace And permanency in my days.

<sup>1</sup> Comfort ye, comfort ye, my people, Saith your God.

<sup>2</sup> Speak ye according to the heart of Jerusalem; And cry to her, That her warfare is accomplished; That her iniquity <span style="color:#800000">fa68</span> is pardoned; For she hath received at the hand of Jehovah Double for all her sins.

<sup>3</sup> A voice crying in the wilderness, Prepare the way of Jehovah; Make straight in the desert a path for our God.

<sup>4</sup> Every valley shall be exalted, And every mountain And hill shall be laid low; And the crooked shall be made straight, And the rough places shall become a plain.

<sup>5</sup> And the glory of Jehovah shall be revealed, And all flesh shall see it together; That the mouth of Jehovah hath spoken it.

<sup>6</sup> The voice said, <span style="color:#800000">fa69</span> Cry; And I said, What shall I cry? All flesh is grass, And all the goodliness of it as the flower of the field.

<!-- PAGE 732 -->

<sup>7</sup> The grass is withered; The flower is faded; Because the breath of Jehovah hath blown upon it. Surely, the people is grass.

<sup>8</sup> The grass withereth; The flower fadeth; But the word of our God shall stand for ever.

<sup>9</sup> Go up into the high mountain, O Zion, that bringest tidings; Lift up thy voice aloud, O Jerusalem, that bringest tidings; Lift it up, be not afraid; Say to the cities of Judah, Behold your God!

<sup>10</sup> Behold, the Lord Jehovah shall come with strength; And his arm shall be powerful. Behold, his reward is with him, And his work is before his face.

<sup>11</sup> He shall feed his flock like a shepherd; He shall gather the lambs in his arm, And shall early them in his bosom, And shall gently lead those that are with young.

<sup>12</sup> Who hath measured the waters in the hollow of his hand, And measured the heavens with his palm, And held with three fingers the dust of the earth, And weighed the mountains in scales, And the heavens in a balance?

<sup>13</sup> Who hath instructed the spirit of Jehovah, Or hath guided him by counsel, And hath taught him?

<sup>14</sup> From whom sought he counsel, that he might advise him? And who taught him the path of judgment, And instructed him in knowledge, And showed to him the way of wisdom?

<sup>15</sup> Lo, the nations are as a drop of a bucket, And are reckoned as the small dust of the balance; Lo, he taketh up the islands as a very little thing.

<sup>16</sup> And Lebanon would not be sufficient to make a fire, Nor its beasts for a burnt-offering.

<sup>17</sup> All nations are as nothing before him, And in comparison of him are reckoned less than nothing, And what hath no existence.

<sup>18</sup> To whom then have ye likened God? Or what resemblance will ye appoint to him?

<!-- PAGE 733 -->

<sup>19</sup> The workman prepareth a graven image, The goldsmith overlayeth it with gold, And (casteth) golden chains.

<sup>20</sup> The poor man chooseth for his offering wood that will not rot; He procureth a skilful workman. To prepare a graven image that shall not be moved.

<sup>21</sup> Do ye not know? Have ye not heard? Hath it not been told you from the beginning? Have ye not been instructed from the foundations of the earth?

<sup>22</sup> It is he that sitteth on the circle of the earth, The inhabitants of which are as locusts; He stretcheth out heaven as a curtain, And spreadeth it out as a tent to be inhabited.

<sup>23</sup> He bringeth the mighty to nothing, The rulers of the earth as if they were not.

<sup>24</sup> It is as if they had not been planted; It is as if they had not been sown; It is as if their stock had no root in the earth. Even while he bloweth on them, they wither, And the whirlwind carrieth them away as stubble.

<sup>25</sup> And to whom will ye liken me, That I may be like? saith the Holy One.

<sup>26</sup> Lift up your eyes on high, And see who hath created those things, Bringing out their army by number; He will call to all of them by name; By the greatness of his strength, And by the might of his power, None shall fail. <span style="color:#800000">fa70</span> <sup>27</sup> Why wilt thou say, <span style="color:#800000">fa71</span> O Jacob? And why wilt thou speak, O Israel? My way is hidden from Jehovah, And my judgment passeth away from my God.

<sup>28</sup> Hast thou not known? hast thou not heard That Jehovah is the God of eternity, Who hath created the ends of the earth? He fainteth not, And is not worn out by fatigue; And there is no searching of his understanding.

<sup>29</sup> He giveth power to the faint, And to him who hath no might he imparteth strength.

<!-- PAGE 734 -->

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>30</sup> The youths are wearied, And they faint; And the young men by falling full.</p>

<sup>31</sup> But they who wait upon Jehovah shall gather new strength; They shall raise their wings as eagles; They shall run, And shall not be wearied; They shall walk, And shall not faint.

<sup>1</sup> Be silent to me, O islands; And let the peoples collect their strength; Let them draw near; then let them speak; Let us come near together to judgment.

<sup>2</sup> Who raised up righteousness from the east, Called him to his foot, Gave nations before him, And subdued kings; Gave them as dust to his sword, And as driven stubble to his bow?

<sup>3</sup> He pursued them; he departed in peace, By the way that his foot had not gone.

<sup>4</sup> Who hath appointed And done it. He who calleth the nations from the beginning. I Jehovah am the first, And with the last I am the same.

<sup>5</sup> The islands saw, And were afraid; The farthest boundaries of the earth trembled; They drew near And came.

<sup>6</sup> Every one brought assistance to his neighbor, And said to his neighbor, Be courageous.

<sup>7</sup> The carver encouraged the founder, And he that beateth with the hammer him that striketh by turns; And he said, It is good for soldering; And he fastened it with nails, that it might not be moved.

<sup>8</sup> But thou, O Israel, (art) my servant, O Jacob, whom I have chosen, Thou seed of Abraham my friend.

<!-- PAGE 735 -->

<sup>9</sup> For I have taken thee from the ends of the earth; From its eminences have I called thee; And I have said to thee, Thou art my servant; I have chosen thee, And have not cast thee off;

<sup>10</sup> Fear not, for I am with thee; :Be not terrified, for I am thy God; For I strengthen thee; yea, <span style="color:#800000">fa72</span> I will help thee; Yea, I will uphold thee with the right hand of my righteousness.

<sup>11</sup> Behold, they who provoke thee, Shall be ashamed And shall blush; They who contend with thee, Shall become as nothing, And shall perish.

<sup>12</sup> Thou shalt seek them, And shalt not find them; They who contend with thee shall be as nothing, And they who fight with thee shall be as consumption.

<sup>13</sup> For I (am) Jehovah thy God, Taking hold of thy right hand, And saying to thee, Fear not, I will help thee.

<sup>14</sup> Fear not, thou worm Jacob, Ye dead men of Israel. I will help thee, saith Jehovah, And thy Redeemer, <span style="color:#800000">fa73</span> the Holy One of Israel.

<sup>15</sup> Behold, I have made thee a cart, Like a sharp thrashing instrument having teeth; Thou shalt thrash the mountains, And beat them small; And thou shalt reduce the hills to dust.

<sup>16</sup> Thou shalt winnow them; And the wind shall carry them away, And the whirlwind shall scatter them. But thou shalt rejoice in Jehovah; Thou shalt glory in the Holy One of Israel.

<sup>17</sup> The needy And poor shall seek waters, Which are nowhere to be seen; Their tongue shall be parched with thirst. I Jehovah will listen to them; I the God of Israel will not forsake them.

<sup>18</sup> I will open rivers on lofty mountain-tops, And fountains in the midst of a plain; I will make the wilderness pools of waters, And the dry land fountains of waters.

<sup>19</sup> In the wilderness I will give the cedar, The sloe, the myrtle, And the pine; I will set in the desert the fir-tree, The elm And poplar together.

<!-- PAGE 736 -->

<sup>20</sup> Therefore let them see And know; Let them likewise think And understand; That the hand of Jehovah hath done this, And that the Holy One of Israel hath created it.

<sup>21</sup> Plead your cause, saith Jehovah; Produce your strong reasons, saith the king of Jacob.

<sup>22</sup> Let them bring them forth, And let them tell us what is to come; Let them declare the former things which were, And we will lay our heart to them; And we shall know the latest of those things; And declare ye to us what is to come.

<sup>23</sup> Tell what shall be hereafter, That we may know that ye are gods; Yea, do good, or do evil, That we may relate And likewise may see <span style="color:#800000">fa74</span> it.

<sup>24</sup> Behold, you are of nothing, And your work is of nothing; (The man) hath chosen abomination in you.

<sup>25</sup> I have raised him from the north, And he hath come; From the rising of the sun shall he call on my name; And he shall come to princes, as to clay; And he shall tread the clay like a potter.

<sup>26</sup> Who hath declared from the beginning, that we may know? Beforehand, And we will say, (He is) righteous. Surely, there is none that declareth; Surely, there is none that causeth (us) to hear; Surely, there is none that heareth your words.

<sup>27</sup> The first to Zion; Behold, behold them; And I will give a messenger to Jerusalem.

<sup>28</sup> I looked, And there was none; I inquired at them, And there was no counselor; I asked them, And they answered not a word.

<sup>29</sup> Behold, all are vanity; And their works are a failure; Their images are wind And confusion.

<!-- PAGE 737 -->

<sup>1</sup> Behold, my servant; I will lean upon him; <span style="color:#800000">fa75</span> Mine sleet, in whom my soul is well pleased. I have put my Spirit upon him; He shall exhibit judgment to the nations.

<sup>2</sup> He shall not cry aloud; He shall not lift up, Nor cause his voice to be heard in the streets.

<sup>3</sup> A bruised reed he shall not break, And the smoking flax he shall not quench; He shall bring forth judgment in truth.

<sup>4</sup> He shall not thine, nor be discouraged, Till he set judgment in the earth; And the islands shall wait for his law.

<sup>5</sup> Thus saith God <span style="color:#800000">fa76</span> Jehovah, The Creator of the heavens, And who stretcheth them out; Who spreadeth out the earth, And its productions; Giving breath to the people who dwell in it, And spirit to them who walk on it.

<sup>6</sup> I Jehovah have called thee in righteousness, And will hold thee by thy hand; And will keep thee, And will place thee for a covenant of the people, For a light of the nations;

<sup>7</sup> That thou mayest open the eyes of the blind; That thou mayest bring out the prisoners from the prison, And from the house of the prison those who sit in darkness.

<sup>8</sup> I am Jehovah, that is my name; And my glory will I not give to another, Nor my praise to graven images.

<sup>9</sup> Behold, the former things have come; And new things do I declare; Before they spring up, I will make them known to you.

<sup>10</sup> Sing to Jehovah a new song, His praise from the end of the earth; Ye who go down to the sea, And its fullness; Ye islands, And their inhabitants.

<sup>11</sup> Let the desert And its cities cry aloud, The villages where Kedar dwelleth; Let the inhabitants of the rock sing; And let them shout from the top of the mountains.

<!-- PAGE 738 -->

<sup>12</sup> Let them give glory to Jehovah, And let them declare his praise in the islands.

<sup>13</sup> Jehovah shall go forth as a giant; And as a warrior shall he rouse his zeal; He shall cry aloud; he shall shout; He shall strengthen himself against his adversaries.

<sup>14</sup> I have kept silence for a long time; I have been silent, And have refrained myself; I will cry like a woman in travail; I will destroy And swallow at once.

<sup>15</sup> I will reduce mountains And hills to a wilderness; I will dry up all their herbage; I will make the rivers islands; And I will dry up the pools.

<sup>16</sup> And I will lead the blind by a way which they knew not; I will cause them to walk in paths which they had not known; And I will turn darkness before them into light, And crooked ways into a plain. These things <span style="color:#800000">fa77</span> will I do to them, And I will not forsake them.

<sup>17</sup> They shall be turned back, With shame shall they be ashamed, Who trust in a graven image; And who say to a molten image; Ye are our gods.

<sup>18</sup> O ye deaf, hear; And ye blind, be attentive to see.

<sup>19</sup> Who is blind but my servant? Who is deaf but my messenger whom I send? Who is blind as he that is perfect, And blind as the servant of Jehovah?

<sup>20</sup> By seeing many things which thou observest not; By opening the ears, so that he may not hear.

<sup>21</sup> Jehovah is well-pleased on account of his righteousness, That he may honor And magnify his law.

<sup>22</sup> But this people hath been robbed And trodden down; They shall all be snared in prisons, And they shall be hidden in caves; They shall be made a spoil, And there shall be none to deliver; A prey, And none shall say, Restore.

<!-- PAGE 739 -->

<sup>23</sup> Who among you will hear this? Who will hearken? Who will attend for the time to come?

<sup>24</sup> Who gave Jacob to be a prey, And gave up Israel to the robbers? Was it not Jehovah? Because we have sinned against him, And they would not walk in his ways, And did not hearken to his law.

<sup>25</sup> Therefore he hath poured upon him The fury of his anger, And the strength of battle. It set him on fire on every side, And he gave no heed to it; It burned him, But he laid it not to heart.

<sup>1</sup> And now thus saith Jehovah, Thy Creator, O Jacob, And thy Former, O Israel; Fear not, for I have redeemed (thee); I have called thee by thy name; thou art mine.

<sup>2</sup> When thou shalt pass through the waters, I will be with thee; Through rivers, they shall not overflow thee; When thou shalt pass through the fire itself, thou shalt not be scorched; And the flame shall not kindle on thee.

<sup>3</sup> For I am Jehovah thy God; The Holy One of Israel, thy Savior; I have given Egypt as the price of thy redemption, Ethiopia And Sheba in thy room.

<sup>4</sup> Because thou hast been precious in my eyes, Thou hast been honorable, And I have loved thee. I will give a man in thy stead, And peoples instead of thy soul.

<sup>5</sup> Fear not, for I am with thee; I will bring thy seed from the East, And I will gather thee from the West.

<sup>6</sup> I will say to the North, Give; And to the South, Hinder not; Bring my sons from a distance, And my daughters from the end of the earth.

<sup>7</sup> All are called by my name, And I have created them for my glory; I have formed them; yea, I have made them.

<!-- PAGE 740 -->

<sup>8</sup> That I may bring forth the blind people, who have eyes; And the deaf, who have ears.

<sup>9</sup> Let all the nations be assembled together, And let the peoples be gathered. Who is there among them to declare this, And to cause us to hear the former things? Let them produce their witnesses, they shall be declared righteous; But let them hear, And they shall says, It is truth.

<sup>10</sup> Ye are my witnesses, saith Jehovah, And my servant, whom I have chosen. Therefore ye shall know And believe me; And ye shall understand that I am He; Before me there was no god formed; Nor shall there be after me.

<sup>11</sup> I, I <span style="color:#800000">fa78</span> am Jehovah; And there is no Savior besides me.

<sup>12</sup> I have declared, And have saved, And have made you to heat; And there is no strange (god) among you. Ye are my witnesses, saith Jehovah, That I am God.

<sup>13</sup> Even before the day was, I was; And there is none to deliver out of my hand. I will do; And who shall disannul it?

<sup>14</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, your Redeemer, The Holy One of Israel; For your sake have I sent to Babylon, And have made them come down. They are all fugitives; And the cry of the Babylonians is in the ships.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>15</sup> I am Jehovah, your Holy One; The Creator of Israel, your King.</p>

<sup>16</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, Who maketh a way in the sea, And a path in the stormy waters.

<sup>17</sup> When he bringeth (them) out, The chariot And the horse, The army And the power, Lie down together, so that they do not rise. They are quenched; they are quenched like flax.

<sup>18</sup> Remember not the former things, And mention not the things that are ancient.

<!-- PAGE 741 -->

<sup>19</sup> Behold, I do a new thing; Now it shall arise; Shall ye not know it? This time <span style="color:#800000">fa79</span> I will make a way ill the wilderness, Rivers in the desert.

<sup>20</sup> The beast of the field shall honor me; The dragons, And the young of the ostrich; Because I will give waters in the wilderness, Rivers in the desert, To give drink to my people, my chosen.

<sup>21</sup> This people have I created for myself; They shall declare my praise.

<sup>22</sup> And thou hast not called on me, O Jacob; But thou hast been wearied of me, O Israel.

<sup>23</sup> Thou hast not brought to me the cattle of thy burnt-offerings; And thou hast not honored me with thy sacrifices; I have not made thee to serve with offering, Nor to be wearied with incense.

<sup>24</sup> Thou hast not bought cane for me with money, And hast not made me drunk with the fat of thy sacrifices; But thou hast made me to serve with thy sills; And thou hast made me to weary with thine iniquities.

<sup>25</sup> I, I <span style="color:#800000">fa80</span> am He, Who blot out thine iniquities for my own sake; Therefore I will not remember thy sins.

<sup>26</sup> Bring to my remembrance; Let us plead together; declare thou, that thou mayest be justified.

<p style="text-align:center" markdown="1"><sup>27</sup> Thy first father hath sinned, And thy teachers have transgressed against me.</p>

<sup>28</sup> Therefore I will pollute the princes of the sanctuary; And I will make Jacob a curse, And Israel a reproach.

<!-- PAGE 742 -->

<sup>1</sup> Yet now hear, O Jacob, my servant, And Israel whom I have chosen.

<sup>2</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, thy Maker, And thy Former from the womb; He will help thee. Fear not, O Jacob, my servant, O beloved, whom I have chosen.

<sup>3</sup> For I will pour waters on that which is thirsty, And floods on the dry ground; I will pour my Spirit on thy seed, And my blessing on thy productions.

<sup>4</sup> And they shall spring up as among the grass, As willows near the streams of waters.

<sup>5</sup> One shall say, I belong to Jehovah; And another shall be called by the name of Jacob; Another shall write with his hand, (I belong) to Jehovah, And shall be surnamed by the name of Israel.

<sup>6</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, the King of Israel, And his Redeemer, Jehovah of hosts; I am the first, And I am also the last; And besides me there is no God.

<sup>7</sup> And who as I shall call, Shall declare this, And set it in order before me, Since I appointed the people of the age? And let them tell them the things that are coming, And the things that shall come.

<sup>8</sup> Fear ye not, And be not terrified. Have I not since then made thee to hear? And have I not declared it? Therefore ye are my witnesses, That there is no God besides me, And there is no strong God whom I know not.

<sup>9</sup> The formers of a graven image are all vanity; And their desirable things do no; profit; And they are their own witnesses That they do not see, nor know; And therefore they shall be confounded.

<!-- PAGE 743 -->

<sup>10</sup> Who is the maker of God? And who is it that hath molten a graven image, Which is profitable for nothing?

<sup>11</sup> Behold, all his companions shall be ashamed; And the workmen themselves are of men; Though they all assemble, And stand up, They shall tremble, And be ashamed together.

<sup>12</sup> The worker in iron shall take the file; <span style="color:#800000">fa81</span> He shall work in the coals; He shall form it with hammers; He shall work in it with the arm of his strength; He shall even be hungry, so that his strength shall fail; He shall not drink water, so that he shall faint.

<sup>13</sup> The carpenter stretcheth out his rule; He marketh it with a dyed thread; He adjusteth it with planes; He shapeth it with a compass; He maketh it according to the shape of a man, According to the image of a man, That it may remain in the house.

<sup>14</sup> He shall cut down for himself cedars; He shall take the pine And the oak; He shall supply himself with trees of the forest; He shall plant a pine which the rain shall nourish.

<sup>15</sup> Then shall a man have them for burning; For he shall take of them, And shall warm himself; Yea, he shall heat an oven, And shall bake bread; He shall also make a god, And worship it; He shall make an idol, And shall bow down before it.

<sup>16</sup> Part of it he shall burn in the fire; Of a part he shall make ready flesh, And shall cat it; He shall roast roast. And shall be satisfied. Next, he shall warm himself, And shall say, Ah! I am warmed, And have seen the fire.

<sup>17</sup> The remainder of it he turneth into a god, Into his graven image; He boweth down before it; He worshippeth And prayeth to it, Saying, Deliver me; for thou art my God.

<sup>18</sup> They have not known, nor understood; For he hath smeared their eyes, that they may not see, And their heart, that they may not understand.

<!-- PAGE 744 -->

<sup>19</sup> It cloth not return into their heart; They have not knowledge, nor understanding, to say, Part of it I have burned in the fire; Yea, on the coals of it have I baked bread; I have roasted flesh, And have eaten it; Shall I turn the remainder of it into an abomination? Shall I bow down before the stock of a tree?

<sup>20</sup> He feedeth on ashes: A deceived heart turneth him aside, So that he cannot deliver his soul, nor sly, Is there not a lie in my right hand?

<sup>21</sup> Remember these things, O Jacob; And Israel, for thou art my servant; I have formed thee; thou art my servant; O Israel, do not thou forget me.

<sup>22</sup> I have blotted out, as a cloud, thy iniquities; As a vapor, thy sins. Return thou to me; For I have redeemed thee.

<sup>23</sup> Praise, O ye heavens; For Jehovah hath done it; Shout, ye lower parts of the earth; Burst forth into praise, ye mountains; O forest. And every tree that is in it; For Jehovah hath redeemed Jacob, And will be glorified ill Israel.

<sup>24</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, thy Redeemer, And thy Maker from the womb: I am Jehovah, who maketh all things, Who stretcheth out the heavens above, Who spreadeth out the earth by my power; <span style="color:#800000">fa82</span> <sup>25</sup> Who frustrateth the tokens of the diviners; Who maketh the soothsayers mad; Who turneth wise men backward; And who maketh their knowledge foolishness;

<sup>26</sup> Who upholdeth the word of his servant, And performeth the counsel of his messengers; Saying to Jerusalem, Thou shalt be inhabited; And to the cities of Judah, Ye shall be built; And I will raise up her ruins.

<sup>27</sup> Who saith to the deep, Be thou dried up; And I will make dry thy rivers;

<!-- PAGE 745 -->

<sup>28</sup> Who saith to Cyrus, Thou art my shepherd; And he shall fulfil all my will; Even saying to Jerusalem, Thou shalt be built; And to the Temple, Thou shalt be founded.

<sup>1</sup> Thus saith Jehovah to his anointed, To Cyrus, whose right hand I have taken hold of, To subdue nations before him; Therefore will I loose <span style="color:#800000">fa83</span> the loins of kings; To open the doors before him; Therefore the gates shall not be shut.

<sup>2</sup> I will go before thee; And will make straight the crooked ways; I will break asunder the brazen doors; And I will hew down the iron bars.

<sup>3</sup> And I will give thee treasures of darkness, And things hidden in secret places; That thou mayest know that I am Jehovah, Who call thee by thy name; Even the God of Israel.

<sup>4</sup> For the sake of my servant Jacob, And for the sake of Israel, mine elect; I will call thee by thy name; I will surname thee, though thou hast not known me.

<sup>5</sup> I am Jehovah, And there is none besides me; There is no god besides me; I have girded thee, though thou hast not known me.

<sup>6</sup> Therefore shall they know, From the rising of the sun, And from the west, That there is none besides me. I am Jehovah; And there is none besides me.

<sup>7</sup> Forming light, And creating darkness; Making peace, And creating evil; I Jehovah do all these things.

<sup>8</sup> Drop down, ye heavens, from above; And let the clouds pour down righteousness; Let the earth be opened; And let salvation And righteousness come forth; Let her bring them forth together: I Jehovah have created it.

<!-- PAGE 746 -->

<sup>9</sup> Wo to him that striveth with his Maker! A potsherd to the potsherds of the earth. Shall the clay say to his maker, Why hast thou made? And, Thy work hath no hands?

<sup>10</sup> Wo to him that saith to his father, Why hast thou begotten And to the woman, Why dost thou bring forth?

<sup>11</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, The Holy One of Israel, And his Maker; Ask me of things to come; Concerning my sons command ye me, And concerning the work of my hands.

<sup>12</sup> I have made the earth, And have created man upon it; It is I, whose hands have stretched out the heavens; And all their army have I commanded.

<sup>13</sup> I have raised him up in righteousness, And will make straight all his ways. He shall build my city, And shall release my captivity; Not for price nor for reward, Saith Jehovah of hosts.

<sup>14</sup> Thus saith Jehovah; The labor of Egypt, the merchandise of Ethiopia, And the Sabeans of great stature, Shall pass over to thee, And shall be thine; They shall walk behind thee; In chains shall they pass over, And shall reverence And beseech thee. Surely <span style="color:#800000">fa84</span> God is in thee; And there is no other besides God.

<sup>15</sup> Truly thou art a God that hidest thyself, O God of Israel, the Savior!

<sup>16</sup> All of them shall be covered with shame, And shall even blush: With shame shall go away together All the makers of graven images.

<sup>17</sup> Israel hath been saved in Jehovah with eternal salvation; Ye shall not be covered with shame; Neither shall ye blush even for ever.

<sup>18</sup> For thus saith Jehovah, who createth the heavens; God himself, the Maker of the earth; Who made it, And furnished it; He created it not empty; He formed it to be inhabited; I am Jehovah, And there is none besides me.

<!-- PAGE 747 -->

<sup>19</sup> Not in secret have I spoken, In a place of the darkness of the earth; Not in vain have I said to the seed of Jacob, Seek me; I Jehovah speak righteousness; I declare what is right.

<sup>20</sup> Assemble yourselves, And come; Draw near together, ye rejected <span style="color:#800000">fa85</span> of the nations. They know nothing, who carry about the wood of a graven image, And who pray to a god who cannot save.

<sup>21</sup> Publish ye, And bring forward; Yea, let them consult together. Who hath caused this to be heard from the beginning? And who hath declared it from ancient times? Is it not I Jehovah? And there is no other god besides me; A just God, And a Savior; There is none besides me.

<sup>22</sup> Look to me, And ye shall be saved, All the ends of the earth; For I am God, And there is none else.

<sup>23</sup> By myself have I sworn; In righteousness hath the word gone out of my mouth, Which shall not return; That to me every knee shall bend; Every tongue shall swear.

<sup>24</sup> Surely in Jehovah, will he say, Have I righteousness And strength; To him shall he come; And all who provoke him shall be ashamed.

<sup>25</sup> In Jehovah all the seed of Israel Shall be justified, And shall glory.

<sup>1</sup> Bel hath bowed down; Nebo is fallen; Their images shall be on the beasts And the cattle; They who carry you are burdened by you; A wearisome burden.

<sup>2</sup> They bowed down; they stooped together; And they could not withdraw themselves from the binden; And their soul hath gone away into captivity.

<!-- PAGE 748 -->

<sup>3</sup> Hear me, O house of Jacob; All ye the remnant of the house of Israel; Who are borne from the womb; Who are carried from the breast.

<sup>4</sup> And even to old age I am the same; And even to gray hairs I will carry. I have made, And I will bear; I will carry, And will save.

<sup>5</sup> To whom will ye liken, And make me equal, And compare me, that I may be like?

<sup>6</sup> Lavishing gold out of bags; Weighing silver in a balance; They shall hire a goldsmith, to make of it a god; To bow down before it, And to worship it.

<sup>7</sup> They shall carry him on the shoulder; They shall lead him about; They shall put him in his place; There shall he stand; From his place shall he not remove; Moreover, if any one cry to him, he will not answer, Nor deliver him from his trouble.

<sup>8</sup> Remember ye this, And blush; <span style="color:#800000">fa86</span> Return to your heart, ye transgressors.

<sup>9</sup> Remember the former things of old; For I am God, And there is none besides, And there is none like me.

<sup>10</sup> Declaring the latest event from the beginning, And from antiquity those things which had not yet been done; Saying, My counsel shall stand, And I will do whatever I wish.

<sup>11</sup> Calling a bird <span style="color:#800000">fa87</span> from the east, From a distant country the man of my counsel; I have spoken, And accordingly I will accomplish it; I have thought, And I will do it.

<sup>12</sup> Hear me, ye that are stubborn in heart, Who are far from righteousness.

<sup>13</sup> I will cause my righteousness to come near, And it shall not be delayed; And my salvation shall not tarry; And I will place salvation in Zion, And my glory in Jerusalem.

<!-- PAGE 749 -->

<sup>1</sup> Come down, And sit in the dust, O virgin daughter of Babylon; Sit on the ground; There is no throne for the daughter of the Babylonians; For they shall no more call thee tender And delicate.

<sup>2</sup> Take millstones, And grind meal; Unbind thy curled locks, make bare the feet; Uncover the limbs, that thou mayest cross the rivers.

<sup>3</sup> Thy baseness shall be exposed, And thy shame shall be seen; I will take vengeance, And will not meet a man. <span style="color:#800000">fa88</span> <sup>4</sup> Our Redeemer, his name is Jehovah of hosts, The Holy One of Israel.

<sup>5</sup> Sit thou silent, enter into darkness, O daughter of the Babylonians; For they shall no longer call thee the mistress of kingdoms.

<sup>6</sup> I was angry with my people; I profaned my inheritance; And I gave them up into thy hand; Thou didst not show compassion to them; On the old man didst thou heavily lay thy yoke.

<sup>7</sup> And thou saidst, I shall for ever be a mistress. Hitherto thou hast not applied thy mind to it, And hast not remembered her end.

<sup>8</sup> But now hear this, thou delicate woman, That sitteth confidently; Who saith in her heart, I am, And there is none besides me; I shall not sit as a widow, And I shall not know bereavement.

<sup>9</sup> But those two things shall suddenly come to thee, Bereavement And widowhood; In their perfection shall they come upon thee, For the multitude <span style="color:#800000">fa89</span> of thy divinations, And for the abundance of thy auguries.

<!-- PAGE 750 -->

<sup>10</sup> For thou trustedst in thy malice; Thou saidst, No one seeth me. Thy wisdom And thy knowledge have led thee astray; And thou saidst in thy heart, It is I, And there is none besides me.

<sup>11</sup> Therefore shall evil come upon thee, the dawn of which thou knowest not crushing shall fall upon thee, which thou shalt not be able to avert; Destruction shall suddenly come upon thee, which thou knowest not; <span style="color:#800000">fa90</span> <sup>12</sup> Stand now amidst thy divinations, And amidst the multitude of thy auguries, In which thou hast wearied thyself from thy youth: If perhaps thou mayest be profited, If perhaps thou mayest prevail.

<sup>13</sup> Thou hast wearied thyself with the multitude of thy counsels; Let the binders of the heavens, The watchers of the stars, who predict by the moon, Stand now And deliver thee, From those things which shall come upon thee.

<sup>14</sup> Behold, they shall he as stubble; The fire shall burn them; They shall not deliver their soul from the strength of the flame; There shall not be a coal to warm, Nor a light at which they may sit.

<sup>15</sup> So shall they be to thee with whom thou weariedst thyself, Thy traders from thy youth. Every one shall wander into his own quarter; There shall be no one to save thee.

<sup>1</sup> Hear this, O house of Jacob, Ye who are called by the name of Israel, Who have come forth from the waters of Judah, Who swear by the name of Jehovah, And remember the God of Israel, Not in truth, nor in righteousness.

<sup>2</sup> For from the holy city they are called, And they rely on the God of Israel, Whose name is Jehovah of hosts.

<sup>3</sup> Long ago did I declare the former things; They went out of my mouth; I published them; I did them suddenly, And they came.

<!-- PAGE 751 -->

<sup>4</sup> For I knew that thou art obstinate; And thy neck is all iron sinew; And thy forehead is of brass.

<sup>5</sup> Long ago did I declare them to thee; Before they came, I showed to thee; Lest perhaps thou shouldst say, My idol hath done these things; My graven image And my molten image hath commanded them.

<sup>6</sup> Thou hast heard; see all things; And will ye not declare them? Even now have I made thee to hear new things, And hidden things, which thou didst not know.

<sup>7</sup> Now for the first time have they been created, And not long ago, nor by a succession of time; Thou hadst not heard them; Lest thou shouldst say, Behold, I knew.

<sup>8</sup> Surely thou hadst not heard; Surely thou hadst not known; Surely it is not long since thine ear was opened; For I knew that by transgressing thou didst transgress; Therefore have I called thee a rebel from the womb.

<sup>9</sup> For my names sake I will delay my fury; And for my praise I will restrain myself toward thee, So as not to cut thee off.

<sup>10</sup> Behold, I have tried thee, And not like silver; I have chosen thee in the furnace of affliction.

<sup>11</sup> For mine own sake, for mine own sake, will I do it; For how shall my name be profaned? And I will not give my glory to another.

<sup>12</sup> Hear me, O Jacob, And O Israel, my called; I, even I, am the first; Also I am the last.

<sup>13</sup> Surely my hand hath founded the earth; And my right hand hath measured <span style="color:#800000">fa91</span> the heavens with the palm; When I call them they stand up <span style="color:#800000">fa92</span> together.

<sup>14</sup> Assemble yourselves, all of you, And hear. Who among them declareth those things? Jehovah hath loved him, And will execute his pleasure against Babylon, And his arm <span style="color:#800000">fa93</span> against the Babylonians.

<!-- PAGE 752 -->

<sup>15</sup> I, I <span style="color:#800000">fa94</span> have spoken; Surely I have called him, I have led him; Therefore he shall prosper in his way.

<sup>16</sup> Draw near to me, hear this. I have not spoken in secret from the beginning; Since the thing was done, there was I; And now the Lord Jehovah, And his Spirit, hath sent me.

<sup>17</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, thy Redeemer, The Holy One of Israel. I am Jehovah thy God, Who teacheth thee profitably, Who directeth thee in the way which thou goest.

<sup>18</sup> O if thou hadst hearkened to my commandments! Then should thy peace have been as a river, And thy righteousness as the waves of the sea.

<sup>19</sup> Thy seed should have been as the sand, And the children of thy womb as the small stones of it; Her name would not be cut off, And would not be destroyed before my face.

<sup>20</sup> Come forth from Babylon; Flee from the Babylonians. With the voice of rejoicing tell this; Publish, And carry it even to the end of the earth. Say ye, Jehovah hath redeemed his servant Jacob.

<sup>21</sup> Therefore they thirsted not when he led them through the deserts; He made water to flow to them from the rock; He clave the rock, And the waters flowed out.

<sup>22</sup> There is no peace, saith Jehovah to the wicked.

<sup>1</sup> Hear me, O islands! And hearken, ye peoples from afar. Jehovah hath called me from the womb; From my mothers belly he hath had my name in remembrance.

<sup>2</sup> And he hath placed nay mouth like a sharp sword; In the shadow of his hand he hath protected me, And hath placed me as a polished arrow; in his quiver hath he hid me;

<!-- PAGE 753 -->

<sup>3</sup> And said to me, Thou art my servant, O Israel! In thee will I he glorified.

<sup>4</sup> But I said, In vain have I toiled; Uselessly And unprofitably have I exhausted my strength; and my judgment is before Jehovah; And my work before my God.

<sup>5</sup> And now saith Jehovah, Who formed me from the womb to be his servant, That I may bring back Jacob to him: And though Israel be not gathered, Yet I shall be glorious in the eyes of Jehovah, And my God shall be my strength.

<sup>6</sup> And he saith, It is a light thing that thou shouldst be my servant, To raise up the tribes of Jacob, And that thou shouldst restore the desolations of Israel; Therefore have I appointed thee to be a light of the Gentiles, That thou mayest be my salvation to the end of the earth.

<sup>7</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, The Redeemer of Israel, His Holy One, To the contemptible in the soul, To the abhorred nation, To the servant of rulers; Kings shall see, And princes shall arise, And shall worship for the sake of Jehovah; For faithful is the Holy One of Israel, who hath chosen thee.

<sup>8</sup> Thus saith Jehovah, In a time of good-pleasure have I listened to thee; In the day of salvation have I assisted thee; And I will preserve thee, . And give thee for a covenant of the people, That thou mayest raise up the earth, That thou mayest possess by inheritance the desolate heritages;

<sup>9</sup> That thou mayest say to them that are bound, Go forth! To them that are in darkness, Show yourselves. On the ways they shall feed; On all the high places shall be their pastures.

<sup>10</sup> They shall not hunger nor thirst; The heat And the sun shall not smite them; For he that hath compassion on them shall guide them; And by the fountains of waters shall he lead them.

<sup>11</sup> And I will place all my mountains for a way; And my paths shall be elevated.

<!-- PAGE 754 -->

<sup>12</sup> Behold! those from afar shall come; And, behold! those from the North, And from the sea; And those from the land of Sinis. <span style="color:#800000">fa95</span> <sup>13</sup> Praise ye, O heavens! And rejoice, thou earth; And break forth into praise, ye mountains; For Jehovah hath comforted his people, And will have compassion on his poor.

<sup>14</sup> Yet Zion hath said, Jehovah hath forsaken me, And my Lord hath forgotten me.

<sup>15</sup> Shall a woman forget her infant, So as not to have compassion oil the soil of her womb? Even though they have forgotten, Yet will not I forget thee.

<sup>16</sup> Behold! on the palms of my hands have I engraven thee; Thy walls are continually before me.

<sup>17</sup> Thy builders hasten; Thy destroyers And demolishers shall depart far from thee.

<sup>18</sup> Lift up thine eyes round about, And see. All are assembled, And have come to thee. I live, saith Jehovah, That thou shalt be clothed with them all, as with an ornament, And shalt be bound with them round about as a bride.

<sup>19</sup> For thy desolations, And thy wastes, And thy ruined land, Shall now be too narrow for the multitude of its inhabitants; And thy destroyers shall depart far away.

<sup>20</sup> The children of thy bereavement shall yet say in thine ears, The place is too narrow for me; Make room for me. that I may dwell.

<sup>21</sup> And thou shalt say in thine heart, Who hath begotten me those? For I am bereaved <span style="color:#800000">fa96</span> And solitary, A wanderer And an exile; Who then hath brought up those? Behold! I was left alone; Whence are they?

<sup>22</sup> Thus saith the Lord Jehovah, Behold! I will lift up my hand to the Gentiles, And will erect my banner to the people; And they shall bring thy sons in their bosom, And thy daughters shall be carried on their shoulders.

<!-- PAGE 755 -->

<sup>23</sup> And kings shall be thy nursing-fathers, And their queens thy nurses; With their faces on the ground they shall worship thee, And shall lick the dust of thy feet; And thou shalt know that I am Jehovah, For they who wait for me shall not be ashamed.

<sup>24</sup> Shall the prey be taken from the mighty? And shall the captivity of the righteous <span style="color:#800000">fa97</span> be delivered?

<sup>25</sup> Yet thus saith Jehovah; Even the captivity of the mighty shall be taken away, And the prey of the tyrant shall be delivered; For I will contend with him who contendeth with thee, And I will save thy children.

<sup>26</sup> And I will feed thy spoilers with their own flesh, And they shall be made drunk with their own blood as with new wine; And all flesh shall know that I am Jehovah, Thy Savior And thy Redeemer, The Mighty One of Jacob.

<sup>1</sup> Thus saith Jehovah: Where is that bill of your mothers divorcement Whom I have dismissed? Or who is the creditor To whom I sold you? Behold! for your iniquities ye have been sold, And to your transgressions was . Your mother dismissed.

<sup>2</sup> Why came I, And no mall (met me)? Called I, And no man answered? By shortening hath my hand been shortened, So that it cannot redeem? Is there not in me power to deliver? Behold! by my rebuke I dry up the sea; I put rivers in the wilderness, So that their fishes purify for want of water, And die for thirst.

<sup>3</sup> I clothe the heavens with blackness, And make their covering like sackcloth.

<sup>4</sup> The Lord Jehovah hath given me the tongue of the learned, That I may know a word in season to the weary. In the morning will he awaken, In the morning will he awaken mine ear, That I may hear as the learned.

<!-- PAGE 756 -->

<sup>5</sup> The Lord Jehovah hath opened mine ear, And I was not rebellious: I did not turn back.
